## How to read this

Once a day the system looks at every name on the list. For each one it reads five kinds of evidence and gives each kind a score from -1.00 (bad) to +1.00 (good). Then it picks a side and says how sure it is, from 0.00 to 1.00.

The three sides: **BULLISH** = the model thinks the price will go up; **BEARISH** = the model thinks the price will go down; **NEUTRAL** = the model has no clear view.

Being sure is not enough on its own. A trade only happens when confidence reaches **0.30**. Below that the system writes down what it thought and does nothing. The size of a trade, the stop-loss and every limit are decided by plain code, not by the model.

Open positions are checked first, before any new trade. When a trade has earned back what it risked (+1R), a third of it is sold and the stop-loss moves up to the entry price, so it can no longer lose. At three times that (+3R) another third is sold and the stop moves up again. The last third stays open. Every day the stop-loss also follows the price up, so a position only ever closes when its stop is hit. The stop only ever moves up.

Under each name you will find the five scores. Click a grey line to open it and see the exact evidence behind that score. The words inside quotation marks are the model's own; nothing there has been rewritten.

### Caterpillar (CAT) · Company — NEUTRAL, confidence 0.45

**In the model's own words:**

> CAT presents a mixed picture with conflicting signals that warrant a NEUTRAL stance. On the positive side, the company has a $72B order backlog approaching annual revenue, fundamentals show exceptional growth (68.2% earnings YoY, 24% revenue growth) with strong profitability (22.2% operating margin, 57% ROE), and analyst consensus is solidly bullish with a 23.3% upside target. However, these positives are heavily offset by technical deterioration: the stock is down 26% from its 52-week high, trading 6.4% below the 50-day MA, with RSI at 42 (near oversold but not yet capitulating), negative MACD, and volume 30% of normal—suggesting weak participation in the 1% daily gain. Valuation remains stretched at 34.14x trailing P/E despite the forward P/E of 24.45, and the stock has declined 10.2% in one month and 16.3% in three months. Recent news mentions 'AI demand fears' meeting the backlog, suggesting sentiment has turned despite operational strength. Insider activity is mixed: one director buying modestly in May, but the CEO sold $26.2M in late August and officers have trimmed positions—not a strong conviction signal from those with best information. The stock appears caught between a strong business story and near-term demand uncertainty, with technicals and sentiment deteriorating faster than fundamentals can support. Earnings on Oct 29 are three weeks away, creating near-term uncertainty.

**Main reasons it gave:**
- $72B order backlog nearly equals annual revenue
- 52-week stock down 26% despite 68% earnings growth YoY
- Stock trading 6.4% below 50-day MA with RSI at 42 and volume at 0.3x average
- Trailing P/E 34.14 remains elevated despite 23.3% analyst upside target
- CEO sold $26.2M stock in August while only one director bought $219K in May

### Microsoft (MSFT) · Company — NEUTRAL, confidence 0.35

**In the model's own words:**

> Microsoft presents a conflicted picture across dimensions. The company is operationally sound with strong fundamentals (17.7% revenue growth, 31.7% earnings growth, 40%+ margins, $16.55B FCF) and has achieved Azure's $100B milestone. Analyst consensus is uniformly bullish with a 15.8% upside to mean target, and the technical trend is constructive (7.5% above 50d SMA, 14.6% above 200d). However, several cross-cutting concerns dampen conviction. The stock has already returned 26% over six months and now sits near 75% of its 52-week range, trading at a 27.6x trailing P/E despite 20.97x forward—suggesting much of near-term upside is priced in. Volume is notably thin at 0.27x average, raising questions about conviction in this rally. News flow is mixed and generic: valuation questions appear (15.5% undervaluation claimed, but separately 'fairly valued' noted), while capital spending concerns resurface (38GW plan by 2032 vs. 3x current capacity). Most critically, insider selling has dominated recent activity—including the CFO (Amy Hood) selling $20.7M just two days ago and CEO Nadella selling $43.4M on Sept 1. While some selling is routine (vesting, diversification), the concentration of C-suite sales recently is a headwind against a crowded consensus long. The technical momentum is also softening: RSI at 54.3 is neutral, MACD has turned negative (histogram -3.199), and the 1-day return is -0.5%. This combination of already-priced-in upside, thin volume, recent insider selling from senior executives, and subtle technical deterioration despite strong fundamentals suggests waiting for a more compelling setup rather than adding here.

- CFO and CEO insider sales totaling $64M in past two weeks
- Volume 27% of 20-day average despite 26% rally over six months
- MACD histogram turned negative while RSI neutral at 54.3

- Azure $100B milestone achieved but stock trailed market all year despite milestone

**In the model's own words:**

> Novo Nordisk presents a conflicted picture across dimensions. Positively: fundamentals remain sound with 35.3% profit margins, 59.8% ROE, and 37.67B free cash flow, alongside a low trailing P/E of 10.32 and analyst price targets implying 11.8% upside. The company is presenting fresh Wegovy pill data at EASD and announced an AI partnership with Anthropic for drug discovery—material catalysts for a diabetes/obesity leader. Negatively: technicals are severely broken. The stock is 7.7% below its 20-day moving average, RSI at 33.8 shows oversold conditions but within a downtrend, and volume is at 0.50x average, suggesting weak conviction in any bounce. The stock has fallen 12% YTD and over 50% in 2025, and recent analyst downgrades from Morgan Stanley, Goldman Sachs, and JP Morgan (three major firms demoting the stock to Neutral or Underweight in 2026) indicate waning institutional support. No insider buying despite the apparent valuation floor provides no confidence that management sees margin of safety. The rebranding effort and obesity drug expansion into children are overshadowed by market skepticism reflected in the 17% one-year decline and cautious hold consensus. News of upcoming data presentations is inherently less surprising than the recent selling-off already price this in. The conflict between cheap fundamentals and broken technicals on a company facing competitive pressure from Eli Lilly in obesity treatment does not justify conviction in either direction.

Earnings on 2026-10-22 are 35 days away but not imminent enough to override near-term headwinds. A NEUTRAL bias with low-to-moderate conviction reflects conflicting signals (consensus bullish vs. fundamentals weakening) and lack of a clear, ticker-specific catalyst.

The combination of defensive positioning (beta 0.49), analyst support, and intact technicals argues against a bearish call, while the recent weakness, elevated valuation, and rising real yields argue against conviction on the upside. First, utility valuations at P/E 19.0 and 2.8% yield remain attractive relative to rising Treasury yields (10-year +12bp on the week to 4.96%), a classic environment where defensive yield plays stabilize. The macro backdrop—rising rates, a normal yield curve, elevated VIX at 16.69, and political uncertainty through the midterms—typically supports utilities as a flight-to-quality vehicle. News of California emissions rule appeals and AI trade skepticism are sector-specific noise, not fund-level catalysts. The weakness is technical exhaustion in a crowded defensive trade, not a fundamental break. A relief bounce from oversold RSI and analyst support is more likely than further deterioration, but conviction remains modest given the ambiguity: technicals are clearly bearish, macro is neutral-to-supportive, and fundamentals lack a fresh catalyst.

This is a wait-for-stabilization setup.

**In the model's own words:**

> CANE (SGG sugar futures fund) shows a modestly bullish technical posture -- price up 20% in 3 months, above both 50d and 200d SMAs, 82nd percentile of 52-week range -- but multiple headwinds prevent a bullish call. Large speculators are at the 100th percentile crowding in a net long position, a state that has historically marked reversals as often as continuations; the recent change is marginal (+0.4% of OI) and offers no confirmation. The cost of holding has been severe: -11.9% annualized drag from rolling futures, a structural penalty that has shown no sign of reverting. Near term, the macro backdrop has shifted materially: USD index up 0.92 on the week, Treasury yields rising across the curve (3-month +14bp, 5-year +17bp, 10-year +12bp), and VIX elevated at 16.69. A stronger dollar and rising real rates are headwinds for commodity prices. Momentum is softening: RSI 54.6 is neutral, MACD histogram turned negative (-0.064), and 1-day and 5-day returns are now underwater despite the longer-term gains. Volume is below average at 0.68x the 20-day mean, suggesting weak conviction in the current position. The case for a near-term pullback outweighs the technical recovery; the extreme crowding and structural carry cost argue against adding to length. No news surfaced in the past 24 hours to alter this balance.

**Main reasons it gave:**
- Speculative positioning at 100th percentile crowding, historically reversals as common as continuations

## Word list

Terms that appear above and have no simpler word:

- **Confidence** — How sure the model is, from 0.00 to 1.00. A trade needs 0.30 or more.
- **R** — The amount one trade risked when it was opened: the distance from the entry price to the stop-loss. +1R means the trade has earned that amount back; +3R means three times it.
- **Score** — How good or bad one kind of evidence looks, from -1.00 (bad) to +1.00 (good).
- **Moving average (SMA)** — The average price over the last N days. A price above it usually means an up trend.
- **RSI** — A 0-100 meter of how fast the price has moved lately. Over 70 means a lot of buying, under 30 a lot of selling.
- **MACD** — Compares a short and a long average to show whether a trend is getting stronger or weaker.
- **P/E** — Price divided by yearly profit per share. A high number means the share is expensive next to today's profit.
- **ATR** — The normal size of one day's price move. The system uses it to place the stop-loss.
- **Stop-loss** — An order that closes the position if the price moves too far the wrong way.
- **Insider** — A director or senior manager of the company. They have to report their own trades.
- **Index fund** — One fund that holds many shares at once, so it follows a whole market instead of one company.
- **Sector or country fund** — A fund that holds many companies, but all of them in one industry or one country. Safer than one company, riskier than a whole-market fund.
- **Positioning** — How much the big professional traders are betting on a commodity or bond, from the weekly US regulator report. High numbers mean the bet is crowded, which cuts both ways.
- **Fund flows** — Money going into or out of a fund. When more people want a fund, new shares are created; when they leave, shares are destroyed. So a rising share count means real money came in. It has already happened -- it is not a forecast.
- **Roll-up** — A fund does not get analyst ratings, but the companies it holds do. A roll-up adds those ratings up, weighted by how much of each company the fund owns. The "coverage" number says how much of the fund that actually covers.
- **Beta** — How hard a fund swings compared with the whole market. Beta 1.0 moves with the market, 2.0 swings twice as hard, 0.5 half as hard.
- **Yield curve** — The gap between what the government pays to borrow for three months and for ten years. Normally ten years costs more. When it costs less -- an "inverted" curve -- markets are expecting a slowdown.
- **VIX** — How nervous the market is about the next month. Under 15 is calm, over 25 is stressed.
- **Build and draw** — A build means more oil or gas went into storage than came out last week, so there is more supply around — usually bad for the price. A draw is the opposite.
- **Good or excellent** — The share of a US crop that government inspectors rate as healthy. A healthier crop means more grain, which usually pushes the price down.
- **Beat and miss** — A company beat if it earned more than analysts forecast, and missed if it earned less.
- **Cost of holding** — A commodity fund does not own the gold or the oil. It owns contracts that expire every month and must be replaced, and the replacement often costs more. That difference comes out of the fund's price every month, even when the commodity itself does not move. Funds that hold real metal in a vault avoid almost all of it.
